# CIRCDIST Gross Round 98 exact review — Round 99

## Verdict

**CLEAR-PROPOSER-LEVEL-CONTINUATION**

I clear the sealed Round-98 package for canonical linking and exact proposer-level reuse. Canonical installation is licensed only by installing the complete replacement YAML in Appendix G, which links this package and review, records the fresh iteration-172 frontier, and preserves the frozen claim, statuses, routes, and history. I did not install it.

The licensed scientific increment is exact but limited: twelve concrete legal schedules at iterations 160--171, twenty-four complete unrestricted direct-360 SAT mechanisms, the exact atomic selector advance 280 to 304, and a finite same-sector, five-distinct-selector no-go over the reviewed post-iteration-136 bases. Strict continuation may begin only from fresh, unstarted iteration 172 with the exact bindings in Section 8.

This review licenses **no** Gross UNSAT, direct absence, repair entry, outer exhaustion, universal schedule cover, distance-12 witness, universal distance-11 ceiling, D-star narrowing, route promotion, or root promotion. `CIRCDIST.gross` and `gap-via-lift` remain `CONJECTURED`; `D*_Gross` remains exactly `{11,12}`.

I was the sole fresh reviewer. I did not contact a proposer or earlier worker, use a sub-agent, delegate, edit the package, scheduler, or canonical node, commit, or push. The repository had 121 pre-existing porcelain-status entries before the one permitted review write. Hidden chain-of-thought is unavailable and is neither requested nor claimed here. The auditable basis is the public formulas, algorithms, source listings, exact commands, hashes, counters, negative controls, and failure ledger below.

## 2. Package census, both seals, inventory, and closure

A recursive regular-file walk made without consulting a manifest produced exactly 1,617 files, 348,878,195 bytes, and 5,005,769 LF bytes. The root manifest has 1,616 rows, the payload manifest 1,615, and the inventory 1,614. Every path is normalized, relative, sorted, and unique. The sets are exact:

```text
root rows      = all regular files - {SHA256SUMS.txt}
payload rows   = all regular files - {SHA256SUMS.txt, PAYLOAD_SHA256SUMS.txt}
inventory rows = all regular files - {SHA256SUMS.txt, PAYLOAD_SHA256SUMS.txt, FILE_INVENTORY.jsonl}
```

I streamed every listed file, recomputed every SHA-256, and compared every inventory byte and LF count.

The live prelink evidence-closure command returned its expected rc=1 with exactly 1,616 unique diagnostics. Every diagnostic path exists and lies below the Round-98 package prefix. The set equals all 1,617 package files except `bindings/initial_proof_dag/circdist/proof-dag/README.md`, which the validator intentionally exempts. The sorted newline-terminated set is 180,729 bytes, SHA-256 `517c462490917f36d5eea55afa332b5effd0c45bfd109462c398190b4236a579`; Appendix H reproduces it. Thus the prelink failure is exact expected evidence debt, not a missing file or foreign unlinked path. The package's stored preseal interpretation has 1,613 diagnostics and is an exact subset; the three later final-seal paths are `FILE_INVENTORY.jsonl`, `PAYLOAD_SHA256SUMS.txt`, and `SHA256SUMS.txt`. After the two new structured evidence entries are present in the disposable overlay, evidence closure passes with 166 structured paths.

## 4. Independent method and command

The principal checker is standard-library-only and imports no package or vendor code. It derives frozen geometry from semantic source JSON, rebuilds both encoders, parses all gzip/JSON/JSONL/DIMACS itself, and calls only the separately authored C++ census binary for the 351-million-tuple stream. Appendix A is the exact source.

## 5. Exact outer source, trace, assertion-map, and CNF reconstruction

For each iteration 160--172, semantic identity is

```text
SHA256(canonical compact JSON(source with semantic_payload_sha256 removed)).
```

The checker rebuilt the definitions from exact Tseitin clauses:

```text
v <-> (a AND b): (-v,a), (-v,b), (v,-a,-b)
v <-> (a OR  b): (v,-a), (v,-b), (-v,a,b)
v <-> (a XOR b): (-a,-b,-v), (a,b,-v), (a,-b,v), (-a,b,v)
```

For rank membership, each output variable gets all 32 truth-table rows over its five pairwise comparisons; output polarity is selected by whether the comparison popcount belongs to the declared position set. The little-endian 10-bit comparator recurrence was separately tested for every `x,y in [0,1023]`, always matching `x<y`.

Root assertions were rebuilt in source order: 864 edge time bounds, X/Z ancilla and data conflict distinctness, all 648 MC equivalences, extrema inequalities and attainment, both global interleaving conditions, 14,484 inherited blocks (1,440 unary, 20 Round-62, 12,820 generalized, 204 dynamic), and every EFSSC exclusion. Definition and root clause IDs are disjoint and partition `1..nclauses`; primary and defined variables are disjoint and partition `1..nvars`; every assertion/map/trace object is accounted for.

Across all thirteen formulas: 249,262 assertions, 5,362,485 variables, 18,211,484 clauses, 5,249,645 definition records, 298,870 root clauses, and 28,408,320 rank cases. The exact aggregate clause-family vector is:

```text

def:and                             8,769,318

def:rank_membership                 1,262,592

root:block:dynamic                      2,652

root:extrema_inequality                22,464
root:global_interleaving                   26
root:mc                                16,848
root:time_upper_bound                  11,232
TOTAL                              18,211,484
```

## 6. Twelve outer SAT models and decoded schedules

Both retained outer assignment containers at each iteration decode to the same complete map: exactly one signed literal for every variable, no duplicate, omission, zero, or out-of-range literal. I evaluated every one of the 16,805,239 clauses; all are satisfied.

For raw time `t(e)`, compression was independently defined as

```text
c(e) = |{u in the used raw-time set : u < t(e)}|.
```

The computed dictionaries equal the stored candidates byte-for-byte. Each decoded schedule has exactly 864 required edges, contiguous nonnegative compressed layers, no ancilla/time or data/time collision, all 648 MC equivalences, and global X/Z interleaving. All prior selectors are false under the candidate source schedule, and the twelve compressed schedule hashes are distinct.

`assignment SHA-256` below hashes the two stored gzip containers in the order package solver / independent extraction.

## 7. Twenty-four unrestricted direct-360 SAT models

For a sector, the checker constructs 360 primary choices: 144 singleton data columns `D:q` and 216 schedule-derived residual columns `R:a:k`. With selection bits `x_j`, each detector equation is

```text
XOR_{j : detector row b meets canonical support C_j oddly} x_j = 0.
```

For each of twelve logical duals it constructs an exact parity output

```text
ell_r <-> XOR_{j : <C_j,L_r>=1} x_j,
```

adds `ell_0 OR ... OR ell_11`, and implements `sum_j x_j <= 10` with an equivalence-checked sequential prefix counter. The reconstructed formula contains no pool, residual-pattern, contributing-check-count, or within-check-order restriction. DIMACS, clause JSONL, variable dictionary, column dictionary, and summary all match byte-for-byte.

Every direct assignment is complete and satisfies every reconstructed clause. For selected names, I independently XORed canonical and schedule-physical supports, derived both syndromes and both logical vectors, and replayed every elementary fault against the physical schedule. In every row: canonical syndrome = physical syndrome = zero; canonical logical = physical logical = the displayed nonzero 12-bit vector. The `named/canonical/physical` triple is deliberately kept separate.

## 8. Atomic selector lineage and exact fresh iteration 172

For each `i=160..171`, source `i+1` is source `i` followed exactly by `r98-i{i}-Xerr` and `r98-i{i}-Zerr`, X before Z. Pending intent and commit payloads are byte-equivalent; state is promoted last; all outputs are present; `repair_entered=false`. The exact appended suffix is:

```text
r98-i160-Xerr, r98-i160-Zerr, r98-i161-Xerr, r98-i161-Zerr,
r98-i162-Xerr, r98-i162-Zerr, r98-i163-Xerr, r98-i163-Zerr,

```

## 9. Exact same-sector order-5 theorem

### 9.1 Input and domain

The synthesis inputs are byte-identical to reviewed Round-96 objects:

| input | SHA-256 |
|---|---|
| `iter137_state.json` | `ba670045a69722f9625d04d3f07d62e207db38f15e9323f8c32d89194bb585cd` |
| `iter136_candidate.json` | `9596d1456ea66c086c06b4b7b4aaf4e442da9943644ae9839d113f10d937a640` |
| `iter136_direct_verification.json` | `f206cb75b7af6cbf2c5421f58c9ebc0c1c0b51d7e44eb3f1c5713a47987a485e` |
| selector state | `47792a8f8e757482604723abb0e08916f49b252f4ba07deae03cf4295159d0b6` |
| source schedule | `45051af0b08e6647d50c6fb0535894173016fd013279e7a0eaee8b2e69ee2e44` |

There are 234 selectors, split into 125 Xerr rows and 109 Zerr rows. The independent row serializers are explicit little-endian and byte-identical to the package: X SHA-256 `2a04ab8d705ad117d2923b6971152256a031e5a4ac528cda886ebe407032e8ff`, Z SHA-256 `26dce46a0779a6d2950cf1f9410704ddd6865bbe9af0e081c11f8d635d13fea1`.

The exact domain is

```text
C(125,5) = 125*124*123*122*121 / 5! = 234,531,275
C(109,5) = 109*108*107*106*105 / 5! = 116,828,271
TOTAL                                      = 351,359,546.
```

### 9.2 Independent enumeration algorithm

The reviewer C++ program starts from `[0,1,2,3,4]`, processes that tuple, then increments the rightmost position `p` satisfying `a[p] < n-5+p` and resets every later position to its predecessor plus one. Termination occurs when no such position exists. The invariant `0<=a<b<c<d<e<n`, the unique successor rule, and the independently computed binomial count prove that every five-element subset occurs once and no repeated-index or permuted duplicate occurs.

For each tuple it XORs the exact linear effect

```text
E = (N[6], C[3], P[3], Sc[2], Sp[2], Lc, Lp),
```

where word counts are 64-bit words for a 360-bit named mask, 144-bit canonical and physical supports, 72-bit canonical and physical syndromes, and 12-bit logical actions. The eligible predicate is `1 <= popcount(N) <= 10`. Equality and multiplicity use `std::map<Key,...>` ordered on all six name words; no hash table or truncated digest participates. The first exact five-index witness and the full remaining effect accompany every key. Appendix B is the exact program.

### 9.3 Exact results and full histograms

| sector | selectors | domain | eligible occurrences | unique eligible | multiplicity histogram | canonical syndrome-zero (full domain) | physical syndrome-zero (full domain) | potentially valid | retained | checksum mod 2^256 |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---|
| Xerr | 125 | 234,531,275 | 38,382 | 38,382 | `{1:38382}` | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | `3a59f0fd8461e5631c7b58583f5ac929fd2072514e931a7020f06d6bc4292e99` |
| Zerr | 109 | 116,828,271 | 1,771 | 1,771 | `{1:1771}` | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | `680db076899136b4baff5d488816f8927c458721383fbcbda3eee49f5f7bdbdb` |

The fresh eligible streams are byte-identical to the package: X SHA-256 `958c864c535d8be6475a0e0deac8bba367386ea50b575817e33c8f9b67da3967`, Z SHA-256 `175397160e26027c0878cbbcdbc6197005de980019417dbe3f82e33bd1c569ec`. Every one of the 40,153 records was decoded, checked for sorted strict indices, recomputed from five independent rows, and replayed at named, canonical, and physical levels. Every miss is exactly `canonical_nonzero_syndrome`; there is no zero-syndrome member anywhere in either full sector domain. Potentially valid = 0 and retained = 0.

Appendices D and E are the complete reviewer-produced histograms. Every named-weight, canonical-support-weight, physical-support-weight, canonical-syndrome-weight, physical-syndrome-weight, and 4,096-bin logical-action histogram sums exactly to its sector's binomial domain. Their SHA-256 values are `1859e67ae54d47ff206c8f88a8984d72c03432a00b64980a74598863531df860` and `c1649b832f1a8cfe18d33a6cc11627f8988168b604cb6b0866a36aede559d946`.

### 9.4 Adversarial controls

* **Multiplicity and collisions.** All 40,153 eligible keys have multiplicity exactly one. Full 384-bit equality is used; hash collision cannot change equality, ordering, or the emitted stream.
* **Overflow.** Domain/histogram/multiplicity counters are `uint64_t`; binomial arithmetic is `__uint128_t` with an explicit `uint64_t` overflow check. Observed maxima are far below `2^64`. Seven independently summed histograms per sector all equal the closed form.
* **Endianness.** Input and output use bytewise little-endian readers/writers. Independently generated input and eligible files are byte-identical to the sealed package, catching host-layout substitution.
* **Repeated indices.** The enumeration proves only strict distinct indices. In GF(2), two equal rows cancel, so a five-term expression with repetitions reduces to lower odd order, but that observation does not extend this theorem; repeated-index scope remains excluded and lower orders rely on their separately reviewed packages.
* **Sector scope.** The common name list is not a license to mix sectors. For selector `iter42-Xerr`, the same ten names yield different Xerr and Zerr canonical/physical/syndrome/logical effects; Appendix C publishes the exact vectors. X and Z rows remain separate.
* **Schedule scope.** `iter42-Xerr` is valid on its source schedule with empty syndrome but invalid on the bound iteration-136 schedule with syndrome `[0,14,15,20,21,31,36,42,43,59,61,62,65,69]`. Source-local validity was not globalized.
* **Weight scope.** Iteration-160 Xerr has named/canonical/physical weights `10/12/12`, proving that named fault count cannot be replaced by either support weight.

The theorem excludes exactly same-sector XORs of five distinct selectors from the 125/109 reviewed bases on the bound schedule. It does **not** exclude mixed sectors, repeated indices, order 6 or higher, orders 1--4 except through separate prior reviewed results, other schedules, other frontiers, arbitrary physical mechanisms, a universal schedule cover, or either D-star branch.

The environment is bound to macOS arm64, Python 3.9.6, z3 5.1.0, pysat 1.9.dev15, no network, and interpreter SHA-256 `bdea59019a38eb6600cc9e71e984a97fedadc406448431281e7657030f54987e`. Every child is exited and reaped; timeout, background, detached, and survivor counts are zero. The 36 SAT proof streams were summarized then deleted under the disclosed positive-SAT policy. Proof-tool controls independently accept valid DRAT, generate and accept LRAT SHA-256 `cf32dfff36b24ba36c3504765f29ecf3d7a8abaa59956ade4d24a4062caa6431`, and reject corrupt/truncated DRAT and LRAT. ## 11. Complete package failure and recovery ledger

Rejected attempts support no claim; packaging recovery added no science.

1. **C++ mixed-`auto` compile, rc=1.** The first C++ source declared three-word and two-word arrays in one `auto` declaration. Clang rejected the unequal deductions at line 39. It produced no census. The declarations were separated and the corrected build returned 0 before any accepted enumeration.
2. **X magic-header abort, rc=-6.** The first X run terminated with `std::runtime_error: magic`. The input was regenerated with exact `CDR98O5V1` header semantics, and both corrected sector runs plus independent byte comparison passed. The aborted stream supports no count.
3. **Wrong vendor directory, rc=1.** The first campaign audit pointed at the package vendor root and failed immediately with `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'model'`. The corrected `vendor/round80` run returned 0. The failed audit supports no assertion.
4. **Three consecutive model-capacity interruptions.** Each occurred after all science, during packaging. Recovery inventories and fresh process censuses show no science child interrupted, no science recomputation authorized or performed, all recorded children reaped, and zero survivors. Recovery only completed metadata, checks, and seals.
5. **Dispatcher prebinding check, rc=1.** `check_round98_protected` was called before `INPUT_BINDINGS.json` existed. Exact bindings were then generated and the check reran successfully. This is packaging order, not science.

The exact package failure ledger is SHA-256 `21ea7201864a65c41aac71405a0da50b41daacee22e19da8c544c2ddf969be45`; its accepted-evidence rule is that only successful corrected runs and independent replays support the result.

The corrected exact-path command, `git status --porcelain=v1 --untracked-files=all -- circdist/reviews/circdist_gross_proof_outer_round98_exact_review_round99_20260831.md`, returned exactly the review as untracked; direct path, byte, and SHA checks then passed. The failed assertion was reviewer bookkeeping after report construction, ran no science, and caused no repository mutation beyond the one permitted review file.

## 15. Exact license and epistemic boundary

A clear proposer-level verdict licenses only these actions:

1. install Appendix G byte-for-byte so the Round-98 package and this review are linked;
2. recognize the already recorded 24 exact selectors and 280-to-304 atomic state chain;
3. continue strictly from the bound fresh/unstarted iteration 172;
4. preserve strict serial solving, both complete unrestricted CSS sectors, MC0, terminal residual semantics, protected inputs, and the proof requirements already stated by the node.

For a future SAT outer result, retain and check the complete model, decode the legal schedule, and adjudicate both unrestricted direct sectors before atomically adding source-schedule-valid selectors. A positive direct mechanism excludes only that candidate. For a future UNSAT result, retain complete DRAT, independently accept it, generate LRAT, and independently accept LRAT with corrupt/truncated controls. Repair remains barred until both direct sectors have checked absence.

Neither twelve SAT candidates nor the finite order-5 no-go closes a branch. The D*=11 branch lacks a universal outer/repair absence proof; the D*=12 branch lacks a schedule with checked direct absence and a certified distance-12 mechanism. Therefore the exact result remains `D*_Gross in {11,12}` and both live claim levels remain `CONJECTURED`.

## Appendix A — exact independent Python checker

SHA-256 `6b493fc9c6a1d753d6141e6e8b1f38504b9e8510de62072f8b9bbc08da8efc11`, 58,636 bytes, 672 LF.

```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Round99 clean-room audit. Standard library only; imports no package code."""
from pathlib import Path, PurePosixPath
from collections import Counter, defaultdict
from itertools import combinations, permutations
import gzip, hashlib, json, math, os, sys, time, gc, struct, subprocess

REPO=Path('<repo>')
R=REPO/'circdist/artifacts/circdist_gross_proof_outer_round98_20260831'
R96=REPO/'circdist/artifacts/circdist_gross_proof_outer_round96_20260831'
OUT=Path('/private/tmp/circdist_round99_exact_review_20260831/cleanroom-result.json')
T0=time.time()

def sha_bytes(b): return hashlib.sha256(b).hexdigest()
def sha_file(p):
 h=hashlib.sha256()
 with Path(p).open('rb') as f:
  for b in iter(lambda:f.read(1<<20),b''): h.update(b)
 return h.hexdigest()
def canon(o): return json.dumps(o,sort_keys=True,separators=(',',':')).encode()
def objsha(o): return sha_bytes(canon(o))
def raw(p):
 p=Path(p)
 if not p.exists(): p=p.with_name(p.name+'.gz')
 b=p.read_bytes(); return gzip.decompress(b) if p.suffix=='.gz' else b
def load(p): return json.loads(raw(p))
def load_jsonl(p): return [json.loads(x) for x in raw(p).splitlines() if x]
def bits(v): return [i for i in range(v.bit_length()) if (v>>i)&1]
def mask(qs):
 v=0
 for q in qs: v|=1<<int(q)
 return v
def parity(v): return bin(v).count("1")&1
def popcount(v): return bin(v).count("1")

def parse_dimacs_bytes(b, keep=True):
 nvars=nclauses=None; clauses=[] if keep else None; seen=0
 for line in b.splitlines():
  if not line or line.startswith(b'c'): continue
  if line.startswith(b'p '):
   _,fmt,nv,nc=line.split(); assert fmt==b'cnf'; nvars=int(nv);nclauses=int(nc);continue
  vals=tuple(map(int,line.split())); assert vals and vals[-1]==0 and 0 not in vals[:-1]
  cl=vals[:-1]; seen+=1
  assert all(0<abs(x)<=nvars for x in cl)
  if keep: clauses.append(cl)
 assert nvars is not None and seen==nclauses
 return nvars,nclauses,clauses

def parse_assignment_bytes(b,nvars):
 vals=[]; status=None
 for line in b.splitlines():
  if line.startswith(b's '): status=line[2:].decode()
  if line.startswith(b'v '): vals.extend(int(x) for x in line[2:].split() if int(x))
 assert len(vals)==nvars and {abs(x) for x in vals}==set(range(1,nvars+1))
 return [False]+[None]*nvars, vals, status

def assignment_map(b,nvars):
 a,vals,status=parse_assignment_bytes(b,nvars)
 for x in vals: a[abs(x)]=x>0
 return a,vals,status

def check_clauses(clauses,a):
 failed=[]
 for j,c in enumerate(clauses,1):
  if not any(a[abs(x)]==(x>0) for x in c): failed.append(j)
 return failed

def parse_manifest(name):
 rows=[]
 for line in (R/name).read_text().splitlines():
  h,sep,rel=line.partition('  '); assert sep and len(h)==64
  q=PurePosixPath(rel); assert not q.is_absolute() and '..' not in q.parts and '.' not in q.parts and q.as_posix()==rel
  rows.append((rel,h)); assert sha_file(R/rel)==h
 assert rows==sorted(rows) and len(rows)==len({x[0] for x in rows})
 return rows

# GF(2) and frozen schedule/direct model, derived only from semantic source JSON.
def rref(rows,n=144):
 rows=[int(x) for x in rows if x]; piv=[]; r=0
 for c in range(n-1,-1,-1):
  p=next((k for k in range(r,len(rows)) if (rows[k]>>c)&1),None)
  if p is None: continue
  rows[r],rows[p]=rows[p],rows[r]
  for k in range(len(rows)):
   if k!=r and ((rows[k]>>c)&1): rows[k]^=rows[r]
  piv.append(c);r+=1
  if r==len(rows):break
 return rows[:r],piv
def inspan(v,rows,n=144):
 rr,piv=rref(rows,n)
 for w,c in zip(rr,piv):
  if (v>>c)&1:v^=w
 return v==0

class Sector:
 RES=((0,1),(3,4,5),(4,5)); PHYS=((2,3,4,5),(3,4,5),(4,5))
 def __init__(self,source,label,times):
  self.label=label;self.times=times; d=source['sector_definitions'][label]
  self.checks=d['checks'];self.kind=d['edge_kind']; self.detrows=[int(x) for x in d['detector_rows']];self.logical=[int(x) for x in d['logical_duals']]
  other=source['z_checks'] if label=='Xerr' else source['x_checks']; self.stabilizers=[mask(x) for x in (source['x_checks'] if label=='Xerr' else source['z_checks'])]
  self.det_of=[[] for _ in range(144)]
  for b,row in enumerate(other):
   for q in row:self.det_of[q].append(b)
  self.dkind='Z' if label=='Xerr' else 'X'
  self.orders=[sorted(row,key=lambda q,a=a:times[f'{self.kind}:{a}:{q}']) for a,row in enumerate(self.checks)]
  self.qorders=[sorted(self.det_of[q],key=lambda b,q=q:times[f'{self.dkind}:{b}:{q}']) for q in range(144)]
  self.columns=[]
  for q in range(144):
   s=1<<q; u=[0];v=0
   for b in reversed(self.qorders[q]):v|=1<<b;u.append(v)
   self.columns.append(self.col(len(self.columns),f'D:{q}',s,s,tuple(u),'data',None,None))
  for a,o in enumerate(self.orders):
   for k,pos in enumerate(self.RES):
    rep=mask(o[p] for p in pos);phys=mask(o[p] for p in self.PHYS[k]);u=0
    for q in bits(phys):
     for b in self.det_of[q]:
      if times[f'{self.dkind}:{b}:{q}']>times[f'{self.kind}:{a}:{q}']:u^=1<<b
    assert self.syndrome(rep)==self.syndrome(phys)
    self.columns.append(self.col(len(self.columns),f'R:{a}:{k}',rep,phys,(u,),'residual',a,k))
  assert len(self.columns)==360 and len({c['support'] for c in self.columns[144:]})==216
  self.byname={c['name']:c for c in self.columns}
 def syndrome(self,s):return sum((parity(s&r)<<i) for i,r in enumerate(self.detrows))
 def col(self,index,name,support,physical,u,kind,check,k): return {'index':index,'name':name,'support':support,'physical':physical,'syndrome':self.syndrome(support),'u':u,'kind':kind,'check':check,'k':k}
 def verify_names(self,names,W=10):
  if len(names)!=len(set(names)) or any(x not in self.byname for x in names):return {'ok':False}
  sel=[self.byname[x]['index'] for x in names];net=0
  for j in sel:net^=self.columns[j]['support']
  syn=self.syndrome(net); pars=[parity(net&x) for x in self.logical]; non=not inspan(net,self.stabilizers)
  return {'ok':len(sel)<=W and syn==0 and non,'weight':len(sel),'selected':sel,'selected_names':names,'net_support':bits(net),'net_weight':bin(net).count("1"),'syndrome':bits(syn),'nontrivial':non,'logical_parities':pars}
 def atoms(self,selected):
  out=set()
  for j in selected:
   c=self.columns[j]
   if c['kind']=='data':continue
   o=self.orders[c['check']]; S=set(bits(c['support']));O=set(o)-S
   if c['k']==0:
    for q in sorted(S):
     for r in sorted(O):out.add((f'{self.kind}:{c["check"]}:{q}','<',f'{self.kind}:{c["check"]}:{r}'))
   else:
    for q in sorted(O):
     for r in sorted(S):out.add((f'{self.kind}:{c["check"]}:{q}','<',f'{self.kind}:{c["check"]}:{r}'))
  assert out
  return [list(x) for x in sorted(out)]

def schedule_audit(source,times):
 edges=set(source['edges']);errors=[]
 if set(times)!=edges:errors.append({'edge_set_mismatch':{'missing':sorted(edges-set(times))[:10],'extra':sorted(set(times)-edges)[:10]}})
 if any(type(x) is not int or x<0 for x in times.values()):errors.append('times_not_nonnegative_integers')
 conflicts=[]; seen={}
 for e,t in times.items():
  kind,a,q=e.split(':');nodes=((kind,int(a)),('D',int(q)))
  for node in nodes:
   z=(node,t)
   if z in seen:conflicts.append([seen[z],e,t])
   seen[z]=e
 used=sorted(set(times.values()))
 if used!=list(range(len(used))):errors.append({'idle_compression':{'used_min':min(used) if used else None,'used_max':max(used) if used else None,'used_count':len(used)}})
 mc=[];pair=whole=0
 for o in source['overlaps']:
  a=o['x_check'];b=o['z_check'];sh=o['qubits'];vals=[times[f'Z:{b}:{q}']<times[f'X:{a}:{q}'] for q in sh]
  if vals[0]!=vals[1]:mc.append([a,b,sh,vals])
  xt=[times[f'X:{a}:{q}'] for q in sh];zt=[times[f'Z:{b}:{q}'] for q in sh]
  if not(max(xt)<min(zt) or max(zt)<min(xt)):pair+=1
  xt=[times[f'X:{a}:{q}'] for q in source['x_checks'][a]];zt=[times[f'Z:{b}:{q}'] for q in source['z_checks'][b]]
  if not(max(xt)<min(zt) or max(zt)<min(xt)):whole+=1
 xs=[times[e] for e in edges if e.startswith('X:')];zs=[times[e] for e in edges if e.startswith('Z:')]
 return {'ok':not errors and not conflicts and not mc,'errors':errors,'edge_count':len(times),'conflicts':conflicts,'used_layers':len(used),'min_time':min(used),'max_time':max(used),'mc_violations':mc,'overlaps':len(source['overlaps']),'global_interleaved':not(max(xs)<min(zs) or max(zs)<min(xs)),'overlap_interleaved_pairs':pair,'whole_check_interleaved_pairs':whole}

# Independent trace/CNF/source checker.
def check_formula(i):
 F=R/f'formulas/iter{i}';source=load(F/'source.json.gz');m=load(F/'metadata.json');tr=load(F/'circuit_trace.json.gz');rows=load_jsonl(F/'assertion_map.jsonl.gz')
 given=source['semantic_payload_sha256'];tmp=dict(source);tmp.pop('semantic_payload_sha256');assert objsha(tmp)==given
 assert m['source_semantic_payload_sha256']==given and m['source_file_sha256']==sha_file(F/'source.json.gz')
 cnfb=raw(F/'outer.cnf.gz'); assert sha_bytes(cnfb)==m['cnf_sha256']; nv,nc,clauses=parse_dimacs_bytes(cnfb,True);assert (nv,nc)==(m['variables'],m['clauses'])
 assert sha_bytes(raw(F/'assertion_map.jsonl.gz'))==m['assertion_map_sha256'] and sha_file(F/'circuit_trace.json.gz')==m['definitions_sha256']
 assert len(rows)==m['assertions']==source['expected']['assertions'] and [x['assertion_index'] for x in rows]==list(range(len(rows)))
 T=tr['constant_variable'];defs=tr['definitions'];assert T==1 and [x['var'] for x in defs]==sorted(x['var'] for x in defs) and len({x['var'] for x in defs})==len(defs)
 used=set(); opcounts=Counter(); defvars=set()
 for d in defs:
  v=d['var'];ids=d['clause_ids'];op=d['op'];inp=d['inputs'];defvars.add(v);opcounts[op]+=len(ids)
  assert not (used&set(ids));used.update(ids)
  got=[clauses[x-1] for x in ids]
  if op=='constant_true':exp=[(v,)]
  elif op=='and':a,b=inp;exp=[(-v,a),(-v,b),(v,-a,-b)]
  elif op=='or':a,b=inp;exp=[(v,-a),(v,-b),(-v,a,b)]
  elif op=='xor':a,b=inp;exp=[(-a,-b,-v),(a,b,-v),(a,-b,v),(-a,b,v)]
  elif op=='rank_membership':
   pos=set(d['positions']);exp=[]
   for mk in range(32):
    mismatch=tuple((-x if (mk>>j)&1 else x) for j,x in enumerate(inp));exp.append(mismatch+((v if bin(mk).count("1") in pos else -v),))
  else:raise AssertionError(op)
  assert got==exp,(i,v,op)
 rootids=set()
 for row in rows:
  ids=row['root_clause_ids'];assert row['root_clauses']==[list(clauses[x-1]) for x in ids];assert not(rootids&set(ids));rootids.update(ids)
 assert not used&rootids and used|rootids==set(range(1,nc+1))
 primary={x for vs in m['time_bit_variables'].values() for x in vs}|{x for vs in m['extrema_bit_variables'].values() for x in vs}
 assert primary|defvars==set(range(1,nv+1)) and not primary&defvars
 # Trace maps and exact roots from source.
 lt={(a,b):v for a,b,v in tr['lt_relations']};eq={tuple(sorted((a,b))):v for a,b,v in tr['eq_relations']};mem={(s,c,tuple(p),q):v for s,c,p,q,v in tr['member_relations']}
 assert len(lt)==len(tr['lt_relations'])==m['comparison_count'];assert len(eq)==len(tr['eq_relations'])==m['word_equality_count'];assert len(mem)==len(tr['member_relations'])==m['membership_expressions']
 ri=0
 def take(fam,sid,css):
  nonlocal ri
  z=rows[ri];ri+=1;assert (z['family'],z['source_id'],z['root_clauses'])==(fam,sid,css)
 for e in source['edges']:
  take('time_nonnegative',e,[]);take('time_upper_bound',e,[[lt['time:'+e,'constant:864']]])
 def distinct(fam,sid,es):
  css=[]
  for a in range(6):
   for b in range(a+1,6):css.append([lt['time:'+es[a],'time:'+es[b]],lt['time:'+es[b],'time:'+es[a]]])
  take(fam,sid,css)
 for a,row in enumerate(source['x_checks']):distinct('x_ancilla_distinct',f'Xcheck:{a}',[f'X:{a}:{q}' for q in row])
 for a,row in enumerate(source['z_checks']):distinct('z_ancilla_distinct',f'Zcheck:{a}',[f'Z:{a}:{q}' for q in row])
 for q,row in enumerate(source['data_incidence']):distinct('data_distinct',f'data:{q}',row)
 for o in source['overlaps']:
  a=o['x_check'];b=o['z_check'];q0,q1=o['qubits'];l0=lt['time:'+f'Z:{b}:{q0}','time:'+f'X:{a}:{q0}'];l1=lt['time:'+f'Z:{b}:{q1}','time:'+f'X:{a}:{q1}'];take('mc_equivalence',f'overlap:{a}:{b}',[[-l0,l1],[l0,-l1]])
 xs=[e for e in source['edges'] if e.startswith('X:')];zs=[e for e in source['edges'] if e.startswith('Z:')]
 for label,es,mode in [('xmin',xs,'min'),('xmax',xs,'max'),('zmin',zs,'min'),('zmax',zs,'max')]:
  for e in es:
   lit=-lt['time:'+e,'extrema:'+label] if mode=='min' else -lt['extrema:'+label,'time:'+e];take('extrema_inequality',f'{label}:{e}',[[lit]])
  take('extrema_attained',label,[[eq[tuple(sorted(('extrema:'+label,'time:'+e)))] for e in es]])
 take('global_interleaving','not_all_X_before_Z',[[-lt['extrema:xmax','extrema:zmin']]])
 take('global_interleaving','not_all_Z_before_X',[[-lt['extrema:zmax','extrema:xmin']]])
 bc=Counter()
 for b in source['blocks']:
  bc[b['family']]+=1;take('reviewed_block',b['source_id'],[[-lt['time:'+x,'time:'+y] for x,y in b['atoms']]])
 assert dict(bc)==source['expected']['block_family_counts']==m['block_family_counts']
 # Reconstruct selector circuit from gate relations.
 gate={}
 for d in defs:
  if d['op'] in ('and','or','xor'):gate[(d['op'],tuple(sorted(d['inputs'])))]=d['var']
 def and2(a,b):
  if a==T:return b
  if b==T:return a
  if a==-T or b==-T:return -T
  if a==b:return a
  if a==-b:return -T
  return gate['and',tuple(sorted((a,b)))]
 def or2(a,b):
  if a==-T:return b
  if b==-T:return a
  if a==T or b==T:return T
  if a==b:return a
  if a==-b:return T
  return gate['or',tuple(sorted((a,b)))]
 def xor2(a,b):
  if a==-T:return b
  if b==-T:return a
  if a==T:return -b
  if b==T:return -a
  if a==b:return -T
  if a==-b:return T
  return gate['xor',tuple(sorted((a,b)))]
 def many(fn,vals,start):
  r=start
  for x in vals:r=fn(r,x)
  return r
 res=[set(x) for x in source['residual_positions']]
 domains=set();sourcekeys=set()
 for si,s in enumerate(source['selectors']):
  sd=source['sector_definitions'][s['sector']];qs=[int(x) for x in sd['qubit_signatures']];ds=0
  for q in s['data_support']:ds^=qs[q]
  terms=[[] for _ in range(84)]
  for cs,kinds in sorted(s['group_kinds'].items(),key=lambda x:int(x[0])):
   p=set()
   for k in kinds:p.symmetric_difference_update(res[int(k)])
   pos=tuple(sorted(p));c=int(cs);domains.add((s['sector'],c,pos))
   for q in sd['checks'][c]:
    sourcekeys.add((s['sector'],c,pos,q));mv=mem[(s['sector'],c,pos,q)];sig=qs[q]
    for bit in range(84):
     if (sig>>bit)&1:terms[bit].append(mv)
  bv=[many(xor2,terms[k],T if ((ds>>k)&1) else -T) for k in range(84)]
  valid=and2(many(and2,[-x for x in bv[:72]],T),many(or2,bv[72:],-T));assert valid==tr['selector_valid_literals'][si]
  take('efssc_exclusion',s['mechanism_id'],[[-valid]])
 assert ri==len(rows) and sourcekeys==set(mem)
 # Rank truth-table clauses imply exact position membership; independently census domains.
 cases=0
 for sec,c,pos in domains:
  row=source['sector_definitions'][sec]['checks'][c]
  for order in permutations(row):
   rank={q:j for j,q in enumerate(order)}
   for q in row:assert (rank[q] in pos)==(sum(rank[x]<rank[q] for x in row if x!=q) in pos);cases+=1
 assert cases==load(F/'encoding_check.json')['rank_membership_local_order_qubit_cases']
 # Family census from actual partition.
 fam=Counter();fam['constant']=opcounts['constant_true'];fam['def:and']=opcounts['and'];fam['def:or']=opcounts['or'];fam['def:xor']=opcounts['xor'];fam['def:rank_membership']=opcounts['rank_membership']
 rootmap={'time_upper_bound':'root:time_upper_bound','x_ancilla_distinct':'root:distinct','z_ancilla_distinct':'root:distinct','data_distinct':'root:distinct','mc_equivalence':'root:mc','extrema_inequality':'root:extrema_inequality','extrema_attained':'root:extrema_attained','global_interleaving':'root:global_interleaving','efssc_exclusion':'root:efssc'}
 for z in rows:
  if z['family']=='reviewed_block': key='root:block:'+z['block_family']
  else:key=rootmap.get(z['family'])
  if key:fam[key]+=len(z['root_clause_ids'])
 assert dict(fam)==m['clause_family_counts']
 out={'i':i,'selectors':len(source['selectors']),'assertions':len(rows),'vars':nv,'clauses':nc,'cnf':m['cnf_sha256'],'source_file':m['source_file_sha256'],'source_semantic':given,'map':m['assertion_map_sha256'],'trace':m['definitions_sha256'],'definitions':len(defs),'root_clauses':len(rootids),'rank_domains':len(domains),'rank_cases':cases}
 del clauses,defs,tr,rows;gc.collect();return source,m,out

formula=[]; sources={}; metas={}
# Exhaust the generic little-endian comparator recurrence independently once.
cc=0
for x in range(1024):
 xb=[bool((x>>j)&1) for j in range(10)]
 for y in range(1024):
  yb=[bool((y>>j)&1) for j in range(10)];z=False
  for a,b in zip(xb,yb):z=((not a and b) or ((a==b) and z))
  assert z==(x<y);cc+=1
assert cc==1_048_576
for i in range(160,173):
 s,m,o=check_formula(i);sources[i]=s;metas[i]=m;formula.append(o);print('R99_CLEANROOM_FORMULA_PASS',i,o['vars'],o['clauses'],o['cnf'],flush=True)
# Sources have exact selector prefixes and frozen base.
base=sources[160]
for i in range(161,173):
 for k in ('blocks','data_incidence','edges','frozen_semantics','overlaps','residual_positions','sector_definitions','x_checks','z_checks'):assert sources[i][k]==base[k]
 assert sources[i]['selectors'][:len(sources[i-1]['selectors'])]==sources[i-1]['selectors']

# Direct CNF encoder independently recreated.
class Encoder:
 def __init__(self):self.vars=[];self.by={};self.cls=[]
 def var(self,name,role,context):
  if name in self.by:return self.by[name]
  v=len(self.vars)+1;self.by[name]=v;self.vars.append({'id':v,'name':name,'role':role,'context':context});return v
 def add(self,lits,rule,context):self.cls.append({'id':len(self.cls)+1,'lits':list(lits),'rule':rule,'context':context})
 def xorlink(self,a,b,y,c):
  for z in ([-y,a,b],[-y,-a,-b],[y,-a,b],[y,a,-b]):self.add(z,'xor2_equivalence',c)
 def xorvalue(self,lits,prefix,ctx):
  assert lits;acc=lits[0]
  for st,x in enumerate(lits[1:],1):
   y=self.var(f'{prefix}.xor_aux.{st}','xor_auxiliary',{**ctx,'step':st,'left':acc,'right':x});self.xorlink(acc,x,y,{**ctx,'step':st,'output':y});acc=y
  return acc
 def xorconstraint(self,lits,p,pre,ctx):
  if not lits:
   if p:self.add([],'empty_xor_contradiction',ctx)
   return
  a=self.xorvalue(lits,pre,ctx);self.add([a if p else -a],'xor_terminal_value',{**ctx,'parity':p})
 def atmost(self,x,k):
  n=len(x);s={}
  for i in range(1,n+1):
   for j in range(1,k+1):s[i,j]=self.var(f'card.s.{i}.{j}','cardinality_auxiliary',{'counter':'prefix_at_least','prefix_length':i,'threshold':j})
  self.add([-s[1,1],x[0]],'card_base_equivalence',{'i':1,'j':1});self.add([s[1,1],-x[0]],'card_base_equivalence',{'i':1,'j':1})
  for j in range(2,k+1):self.add([-s[1,j]],'card_impossible_base',{'i':1,'j':j})
  for i in range(2,n+1):
   xi=x[i-1];y=s[i,1];a=s[i-1,1]
   self.add([-y,a,xi],'card_or_equivalence',{'i':i,'j':1});self.add([-a,y],'card_or_equivalence',{'i':i,'j':1});self.add([-xi,y],'card_or_equivalence',{'i':i,'j':1})
   for j in range(2,k+1):
    y=s[i,j];a=s[i-1,j];b=s[i-1,j-1];c={'i':i,'j':j}
    self.add([-y,a,b],'card_recurrence_equivalence',c);self.add([-y,a,xi],'card_recurrence_equivalence',c);self.add([-a,y],'card_recurrence_equivalence',c);self.add([-b,-xi,y],'card_recurrence_equivalence',c)
   self.add([-s[i-1,k],-xi],'card_atmost_overflow_forbidden',{'i':i,'bound':k})

def direct_expected(sec,candsha):
 E=Encoder();x=[E.var(f'select[{c["index"]}]','direct_column_selection',{'column_index':c['index'],'column_name':c['name']}) for c in sec.columns];assert x==list(range(1,361))
 synrows=[]
 for b in range(72):
  lits=[x[c['index']] for c in sec.columns if (c['syndrome']>>b)&1];synrows.append({'row':b,'literals':lits,'column_indices':[v-1 for v in lits]});E.xorconstraint(lits,0,f'syndrome[{b}]',{'family':'detector_syndrome','row':b})
 logrows=[];lvars=[]
 for r,l in enumerate(sec.logical):
  lits=[x[c['index']] for c in sec.columns if parity(c['support']&l)];lv=E.var(f'logical_parity[{r}]','logical_parity_output',{'logical_dual_index':r,'logical_dual_support':bits(l)});acc=E.xorvalue(lits,f'logical[{r}]',{'family':'logical_parity','logical_dual_index':r});E.add([-lv,acc],'logical_output_equivalence',{'logical_dual_index':r});E.add([lv,-acc],'logical_output_equivalence',{'logical_dual_index':r});lvars.append(lv);logrows.append({'row':r,'logical_dual_support':bits(l),'logical_dual_mask_hex':hex(l),'literals':lits,'column_indices':[v-1 for v in lits],'output_var':lv})
 E.add(lvars,'logical_nonzero_disjunction',{'logical_outputs':lvars});E.atmost(x,10)
 lines=[f'c CIRCDIST Gross direct-360 {sec.label} W<=10',f'c candidate_sha256 {candsha}','c variables 1..360 select the exact columns listed in the companion column dictionary',f'p cnf {len(E.vars)} {len(E.cls)}']+[' '.join(map(str,c['lits']))+' 0' for c in E.cls];dimacs=('\n'.join(lines)+'\n').encode();body=''.join(' '.join(map(str,c['lits']))+' 0\n' for c in E.cls).encode()
 return E,dimacs,synrows,logrows,sha_bytes(body)

def columns_public(sec,candsha,synrows,logrows):
 cols=[]
 for c in sec.columns:
  cols.append({'index':c['index'],'primary_var':c['index']+1,'name':c['name'],'kind':c['kind'],'check':c['check'],'residual_kind':c['k'],'support':bits(c['support']),'support_mask_hex':hex(c['support']),'physical_support':bits(c['physical']),'syndrome':bits(c['syndrome']),'syndrome_mask_hex':hex(c['syndrome']),'u_options':[{'bits':bits(u),'mask_hex':hex(u)} for u in c['u']]})
 return {'schema':'circdist-gross-direct360-column-dictionary-v1','sector':sec.label,'candidate_sha256':candsha,'column_count':360,'columns':cols,'detector_rows':[{'row':i,'support':bits(v),'mask_hex':hex(v)} for i,v in enumerate(sec.detrows)],'stabilizer_rows':[{'row':i,'support':bits(v),'mask_hex':hex(v)} for i,v in enumerate(sec.stabilizers)],'logical_duals':[{'row':i,'support':bits(v),'mask_hex':hex(v)} for i,v in enumerate(sec.logical)],'syndrome_equations':synrows,'logical_equations':logrows}

def words(v,n):return [(v>>(64*i))&((1<<64)-1) for i in range(n)]
def fromwords(ws):return sum(int(x)<<(64*i) for i,x in enumerate(ws))
def logical_mask(sec,support):return sum(parity(support&d)<<i for i,d in enumerate(sec.logical))
def effect(sec,names):
 can=phys=0
 for name in names:can^=sec.byname[name]['support'];phys^=sec.byname[name]['physical']

  nm=sum(1<<name_bit[x] for x in selector['selected_names']);can,phys,cs,ps,cl,pl=effect(synth_sectors[label],selector['selected_names'])

  assert len(vals)==19;rows.append((selector,vals,(nm,can,phys,cs,ps,cl,pl)));ids.append(selector['mechanism_id'])

## Appendix B — exact independent C++ order-5 enumerator

SHA-256 `8e46edbd7858c955da5b9ae2e07c45d49810db19a8898767210365369bd0af7d`, 11,239 bytes, 172 LF.

```cpp
#include <array>
#include <algorithm>
#include <cstdint>
#include <fstream>
#include <iomanip>
#include <iostream>
#include <map>
#include <sstream>
#include <stdexcept>
#include <string>
#include <vector>

using U = std::uint64_t;
struct Row {
  U global_index;
  std::array<U,6> names;
  std::array<U,3> canonical, physical;
  std::array<U,2> canonical_syndrome, physical_syndrome;
  U canonical_logical, physical_logical;
};
struct Key {
  std::array<U,6> words;
  bool operator<(Key const& other) const { return words < other.words; }
  bool operator==(Key const& other) const { return words == other.words; }
};
struct Entry {
  std::array<U,3> canonical{}, physical{};
  std::array<U,2> canonical_syndrome{}, physical_syndrome{};
  U canonical_logical=0, physical_logical=0, multiplicity=0;
  std::array<U,5> witness{};
};

static U choose(U n,U k) {
  if (k>n) return 0;
  if (k>n-k) k=n-k;
  __uint128_t value=1;
  for (U i=1;i<=k;i++) value=value*(n-k+i)/i;
  if (value>~U(0)) throw std::overflow_error("binomial overflow");
  return U(value);
}
static U read_le64(std::istream& in) {
  unsigned char b[8]; in.read(reinterpret_cast<char*>(b),8);
  if (!in) throw std::runtime_error("truncated little-endian input");
  U x=0; for (int i=0;i<8;i++) x |= U(b[i]) << (8*i); return x;
}
static void write_le64(std::ostream& out,U x) {
  unsigned char b[8]; for (int i=0;i<8;i++) b[i]=unsigned((x>>(8*i))&255);
  out.write(reinterpret_cast<char*>(b),8);
}
template<std::size_t N> static std::array<U,N> read_array(std::istream& in) {
  std::array<U,N> a{}; for (auto& x:a) x=read_le64(in); return a;
}
template<std::size_t N> static void write_array(std::ostream& out,std::array<U,N> const& a) {
  for (U x:a) write_le64(out,x);
}
template<std::size_t N> static std::array<U,N> xor_five(
    std::array<U,N> const&a,std::array<U,N> const&b,std::array<U,N> const&c,
    std::array<U,N> const&d,std::array<U,N> const&e) {
  std::array<U,N> z{}; for (std::size_t i=0;i<N;i++) z[i]=a[i]^b[i]^c[i]^d[i]^e[i]; return z;
}
static unsigned pop(U x) { return unsigned(__builtin_popcountll(x)); }
template<std::size_t N> static unsigned pop(std::array<U,N> const& a) {
  unsigned z=0; for (U x:a) z+=pop(x); return z;
}
static bool next_combination(std::array<U,5>& a,U n) {
  for (int p=4;p>=0;p--) {
    U limit=n-5+U(p);
    if (a[p]<limit) {
      ++a[p]; for (int q=p+1;q<5;q++) a[q]=a[q-1]+1; return true;
    }
  }
  return false;
}
static void add256(std::array<U,4>& acc,U x,unsigned limb) {
  __uint128_t z=__uint128_t(acc[limb])+x; acc[limb]=U(z); U carry=U(z>>64);
  for (unsigned i=limb+1;carry && i<4;i++) { z=__uint128_t(acc[i])+carry; acc[i]=U(z); carry=U(z>>64); }
}
static std::string json_array(std::vector<U> const& a) {
  std::ostringstream s; s<<'['; for(std::size_t i=0;i<a.size();i++){if(i)s<<',';s<<a[i];} s<<']'; return s.str();
}
static std::string hex256(std::array<U,4> const& a) {
  std::ostringstream s; s<<std::hex<<std::setfill('0'); for(int i=3;i>=0;i--) s<<std::setw(16)<<a[i]; return s.str();
}
static void require_magic(std::istream& in,char const* stem) {
  unsigned char got[16], want[16]{}; in.read(reinterpret_cast<char*>(got),16); if(!in) throw std::runtime_error("missing magic");
  for(int i=0;i<9;i++) want[i]=unsigned(stem[i]);
  if(!std::equal(got,got+16,want)) throw std::runtime_error("wrong magic");
}
static void write_magic(std::ostream& out,char const* stem) {
  unsigned char b[16]{}; for(int i=0;i<9;i++) b[i]=unsigned(stem[i]); out.write(reinterpret_cast<char*>(b),16);
}
int main(int argc,char**argv) try {
  if(argc!=5){std::cerr<<"usage: r99_order5_enumerator INPUT ELIGIBLE SUMMARY SECTOR\n";return 2;}
  std::ifstream input(argv[1],std::ios::binary); if(!input) throw std::runtime_error("cannot open input");
  require_magic(input,"CDR98O5V1"); U n=read_le64(input); if(n<5 || n>10000) throw std::runtime_error("invalid selector count");
  std::vector<Row> rows(n);
  for(auto& r:rows){r.global_index=read_le64(input);r.names=read_array<6>(input);r.canonical=read_array<3>(input);r.physical=read_array<3>(input);r.canonical_syndrome=read_array<2>(input);r.physical_syndrome=read_array<2>(input);r.canonical_logical=read_le64(input);r.physical_logical=read_le64(input);}
  char trailing; if(input.read(&trailing,1)) throw std::runtime_error("trailing input byte");
  std::vector<U> named_hist(51),canonical_hist(145),physical_hist(145),canonical_syn_hist(73),physical_syn_hist(73),canonical_logical_hist(4096),physical_logical_hist(4096);
  U visited=0,zero_named=0,zero_canonical=0,zero_physical=0,zero_csyn=0,zero_psyn=0,zero_clog=0,zero_plog=0,canonical_valid=0,physical_valid=0,conservative_valid=0,eligible_occurrences=0;
  std::array<U,4> checksum{};
  std::map<Key,Entry> eligible; // ordered full 384-bit keys: no hashing anywhere
  std::array<U,5> ix{0,1,2,3,4};
  do {
    auto const&A=rows[ix[0]],&B=rows[ix[1]],&C=rows[ix[2]],&D=rows[ix[3]],&E=rows[ix[4]];
    Key key{xor_five(A.names,B.names,C.names,D.names,E.names)};
    auto can=xor_five(A.canonical,B.canonical,C.canonical,D.canonical,E.canonical);
    auto phys=xor_five(A.physical,B.physical,C.physical,D.physical,E.physical);
    auto cs=xor_five(A.canonical_syndrome,B.canonical_syndrome,C.canonical_syndrome,D.canonical_syndrome,E.canonical_syndrome);
    auto ps=xor_five(A.physical_syndrome,B.physical_syndrome,C.physical_syndrome,D.physical_syndrome,E.physical_syndrome);
    U cl=A.canonical_logical^B.canonical_logical^C.canonical_logical^D.canonical_logical^E.canonical_logical;
    U pl=A.physical_logical^B.physical_logical^C.physical_logical^D.physical_logical^E.physical_logical;
    unsigned w=pop(key.words),cw=pop(can),pw=pop(phys),csw=pop(cs),psw=pop(ps);
    if(w>50||cw>144||pw>144||csw>72||psw>72||cl>=4096||pl>=4096) throw std::runtime_error("effect outside declared dimensions");
    ++visited;++named_hist[w];++canonical_hist[cw];++physical_hist[pw];++canonical_syn_hist[csw];++physical_syn_hist[psw];++canonical_logical_hist[cl];++physical_logical_hist[pl];
    zero_named+=(w==0);zero_canonical+=(cw==0);zero_physical+=(pw==0);zero_csyn+=(csw==0);zero_psyn+=(psw==0);zero_clog+=(cl==0);zero_plog+=(pl==0);
    bool cv=(w>=1&&w<=10&&csw==0&&cl!=0),pv=(w>=1&&w<=10&&psw==0&&pl!=0),cons=(cv&&pv&&cl==pl);
    canonical_valid+=cv;physical_valid+=pv;conservative_valid+=cons;
    U q=(ix[0]+1)*3+(ix[1]+1)*5+(ix[2]+1)*7+(ix[3]+1)*11+(ix[4]+1)*13;add256(checksum,q,0);
    for(std::size_t j=0;j<6;j++)add256(checksum,key.words[j],unsigned(j&3));
    for(std::size_t j=0;j<3;j++){add256(checksum,can[j],unsigned((j+1)&3));add256(checksum,phys[j],unsigned((j+2)&3));}
    for(std::size_t j=0;j<2;j++){add256(checksum,cs[j],unsigned((j+1)&3));add256(checksum,ps[j],unsigned((j+2)&3));}
    add256(checksum,cl,2);add256(checksum,pl,3);
    if(w>=1&&w<=10){
      ++eligible_occurrences; auto [it,inserted]=eligible.try_emplace(key);
      auto& e=it->second;
      if(inserted){e.canonical=can;e.physical=phys;e.canonical_syndrome=cs;e.physical_syndrome=ps;e.canonical_logical=cl;e.physical_logical=pl;e.multiplicity=1;e.witness=ix;}
      else {if(e.canonical!=can||e.physical!=phys||e.canonical_syndrome!=cs||e.physical_syndrome!=ps||e.canonical_logical!=cl||e.physical_logical!=pl)throw std::runtime_error("same named mask has inconsistent linear effect");++e.multiplicity;}
    }
  } while(next_combination(ix,n));
  U expected=choose(n,5); if(visited!=expected) throw std::runtime_error("combination count mismatch");
  for(auto const* h:{&named_hist,&canonical_hist,&physical_hist,&canonical_syn_hist,&physical_syn_hist,&canonical_logical_hist,&physical_logical_hist}){U sum=0;for(U x:*h)sum+=x;if(sum!=visited)throw std::runtime_error("histogram total mismatch");}
  std::ofstream ef(argv[2],std::ios::binary); if(!ef)throw std::runtime_error("cannot open eligible output");write_magic(ef,"CDR98E5V1");write_le64(ef,U(eligible.size()));
  std::map<U,U> multiplicities;U maximum_multiplicity=0;
  for(auto const& kv:eligible){auto const& k=kv.first;auto const&e=kv.second;write_array(ef,k.words);write_array(ef,e.canonical);write_array(ef,e.physical);write_array(ef,e.canonical_syndrome);write_array(ef,e.physical_syndrome);write_le64(ef,e.canonical_logical);write_le64(ef,e.physical_logical);write_le64(ef,e.multiplicity);write_array(ef,e.witness);++multiplicities[e.multiplicity];maximum_multiplicity=std::max(maximum_multiplicity,e.multiplicity);}ef.close();if(!ef)throw std::runtime_error("eligible output write failed");
  std::vector<U> multiplicity_flat;for(auto const&kv:multiplicities){multiplicity_flat.push_back(kv.first);multiplicity_flat.push_back(kv.second);}
  std::ofstream out(argv[3]);if(!out)throw std::runtime_error("cannot open summary output");
  out<<"{\n"
     <<"  \"schema\": \"circdist-round99-independent-order5-census-v1\",\n"
     <<"  \"status\": \"PASS\",\n"
     <<"  \"sector\": \""<<argv[4]<<"\",\n"
     <<"  \"selector_count\": "<<n<<",\n"
     <<"  \"domain_expected\": "<<expected<<",\n"
     <<"  \"domain_enumerated\": "<<visited<<",\n"
     <<"  \"location_weight_histogram\": "<<json_array(named_hist)<<",\n"
     <<"  \"canonical_support_weight_histogram\": "<<json_array(canonical_hist)<<",\n"
     <<"  \"physical_support_weight_histogram\": "<<json_array(physical_hist)<<",\n"
     <<"  \"canonical_syndrome_weight_histogram\": "<<json_array(canonical_syn_hist)<<",\n"
     <<"  \"physical_syndrome_weight_histogram\": "<<json_array(physical_syn_hist)<<",\n"
     <<"  \"canonical_logical_action_histogram\": "<<json_array(canonical_logical_hist)<<",\n"
     <<"  \"physical_logical_action_histogram\": "<<json_array(physical_logical_hist)<<",\n"
     <<"  \"zero_named_support\": "<<zero_named<<",\n"
     <<"  \"zero_canonical_support\": "<<zero_canonical<<",\n"
     <<"  \"zero_physical_support\": "<<zero_physical<<",\n"
     <<"  \"canonical_syndrome_zero\": "<<zero_csyn<<",\n"
     <<"  \"physical_syndrome_zero\": "<<zero_psyn<<",\n"
     <<"  \"canonical_logical_trivial\": "<<zero_clog<<",\n"
     <<"  \"physical_logical_trivial\": "<<zero_plog<<",\n"
     <<"  \"canonical_source_valid_occurrences\": "<<canonical_valid<<",\n"
     <<"  \"physical_source_valid_occurrences\": "<<physical_valid<<",\n"
     <<"  \"conservative_source_valid_occurrences\": "<<conservative_valid<<",\n"
     <<"  \"eligible_weight_1_to_10_occurrences\": "<<eligible_occurrences<<",\n"
     <<"  \"unique_eligible_named_results\": "<<eligible.size()<<",\n"
     <<"  \"multiplicity_histogram_flat_pairs\": "<<json_array(multiplicity_flat)<<",\n"
     <<"  \"maximum_eligible_multiplicity\": "<<maximum_multiplicity<<",\n"
     <<"  \"enumeration_checksum_mod_2_256\": \""<<hex256(checksum)<<"\",\n"
     <<"  \"unordered_indexing\": \"lexicographic next-combination traversal with strict a<b<c<d<e\",\n"
     <<"  \"key_semantics\": \"std::map ordered by all six 64-bit words; no hash function is used\"\n"
     <<"}\n";out.close();if(!out)throw std::runtime_error("summary output write failed");
  std::cout<<"R99_ORDER5_ENUM_PASS "<<argv[4]<<' '<<n<<' '<<visited<<' '<<eligible_occurrences<<' '<<eligible.size()<<' '<<conservative_valid<<"\n";
  return 0;
} catch(std::exception const&e) { std::cerr<<"R99_ORDER5_ENUM_FAIL "<<e.what()<<"\n"; return 1; }
```

## Appendix F — dependency checker/result and overlay record

### F.1 Round-96 dependency checker

```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
from pathlib import Path,PurePosixPath
import hashlib,json
R=Path('<repo>/circdist/artifacts/circdist_gross_proof_outer_round96_20260831')
def sha(p):
 h=hashlib.sha256()
 with p.open('rb') as f:
  for b in iter(lambda:f.read(1<<20),b''):h.update(b)
 return h.hexdigest()
files=sorted((p for p in R.rglob('*') if p.is_file()),key=lambda p:p.relative_to(R).as_posix()); actual={p.relative_to(R).as_posix() for p in files}
def manifest(n):
 rows=[]
 for line in (R/n).read_text().splitlines():
  h,sep,q=line.partition('  '); assert sep and len(h)==64 and PurePosixPath(q).as_posix()==q and not PurePosixPath(q).is_absolute() and '..' not in PurePosixPath(q).parts
  assert sha(R/q)==h;rows.append((q,h))
 assert rows==sorted(rows) and len(rows)==len({q for q,h in rows});return rows
a=manifest('SHA256SUMS.txt');b=manifest('PAYLOAD_SHA256SUMS.txt')
assert {q for q,h in a}==actual-{'SHA256SUMS.txt'}
assert {q for q,h in b}==actual-{'SHA256SUMS.txt','PAYLOAD_SHA256SUMS.txt'}
inv=[json.loads(x) for x in (R/'FILE_INVENTORY.jsonl').read_text().splitlines() if x]
assert [x['path'] for x in inv]==sorted(x['path'] for x in inv) and len(inv)==len({x['path'] for x in inv})
assert {x['path'] for x in inv}==actual-{'SHA256SUMS.txt','PAYLOAD_SHA256SUMS.txt','FILE_INVENTORY.jsonl'}
for x in inv:
 z=(R/x['path']).read_bytes();assert (sha(R/x['path']),len(z),z.count(b'\n'))==(x['sha256'],x['bytes'],x['lines'])
out={'schema':'round99-reviewed-round96-dependency-v1','status':'PASS','files':len(files),'bytes':sum(p.stat().st_size for p in files),'lf':sum(p.read_bytes().count(b'\n') for p in files),'root_rows':len(a),'payload_rows':len(b),'inventory_rows':len(inv),'root':sha(R/'SHA256SUMS.txt'),'payload':sha(R/'PAYLOAD_SHA256SUMS.txt'),'inventory':sha(R/'FILE_INVENTORY.jsonl')}
assert out|{}==out
z=(json.dumps(out,indent=2,sort_keys=True)+'\n').encode();Path('/private/tmp/circdist_round99_exact_review_20260831/round96_dependency_result.json').write_bytes(z);print(z.decode(),end='')
```

## Appendix G — complete byte-exact proposed replacement `CIRCDIST.gross.yaml`

This is the sole installation artifact licensed by this review. Extracting the bytes between this `yaml` fence and its closing fence, including the final LF before the fence, yields SHA-256 `43abf1f383f44a4aee98c71da17af6fddca8b526d76cf135cd16c89a9aaae14c`, 85,062 bytes, 938 LF. This is a full document, not a patch or excerpt. It was not installed by this reviewer.

```yaml
id: CIRCDIST.gross
role: outcome
aliases:
- GROSS-DSTAR
claim: For the Gross code [[144,12,12]] with single-ancilla CNOT syndrome extraction (interleaving of X- and Z-check CNOTs
  allowed; each required CNOT exactly once; one two-qubit gate per qubit per time step; no flag ancillas, cat states, or generating-set
  changes; idle layers compressed), the optimum D*_Gross = max over legal schedules M of d_circ(C(M)) is determined with proof,
  as either an explicit interleaved schedule with certified d_circ = 12, or a proof that every legal schedule satisfies d_circ
  <= 11; and the published conjectured distance-11 circuit has a certified d_circ = 11.
assessment:
  level: CONJECTURED
  caveats:
  - Do not cite SBB26 Appendix H.2 as proving circuit distance 12 for the original Gross code. Shaw-Terhal Table I reports
    only tilde d_circ <= 12 for a different end-cycle “second gross” code; equality of n=144 and k=12 (or of the 288-qubit
    total hardware count) is not code identity or S_Gross membership.
  summary: 'The optimal circuit-distance question remains CONJECTURED, with D* in {11,12} unresolved. The universal d_ext
    <= 11 cap is PROVED; the candidate-certification route is CERTIFIED*. The latest completion-gap candidate was killed by
    the missed two-prefix shape. The three recorded no-go classes and search machinery are scoped by-products, not a universal
    proof of D*=11. A fresh primary-source scope audit closes the Shaw-Terhal morphing lead negatively: its distance-12-labeled
    endpoint is a different weight-9 [[144,12,12]] “second gross” code with alternating code frames, outside frozen S_Gross,
    and Table I gives only a BP-OSD circuit-distance upper bound <=12. It supplies neither a legal distance-12 witness nor
    a universal <=11 no-go. Round 61''s proposer-level clean-room audit refutes two further completion screens: absence of
    all four individually gain-positive shapes does not exclude cancelling multi-check weight-10 mechanisms, and even a clean
    |V|=12 / at-most-four-check stratum with diagonal short hooks does not exclude repair-zero minimum mechanisms. It also
    finds that hunt2''s j<=4 recursion did not enforce distinct contributing checks; the earlier candidate kill survives because
    its 144 decisive witnesses were separately rebuilt from raw schedules. These are filter no-go results, not a universal
    theorem, so D* remains in {11,12}. Round 62 installs a proposer-level unrestricted 864-edge/648-MC CEGAR package with
    direct-360 effective and arbitrary-round repair adversaries plus raw replay. Its first 20 exact outer schedules are all
    legal, MC-valid and interleaved, and all are killed in Xerr by verified weight-10 mechanisms; 20 sound order-region blocks
    and all 190 prior-block exclusions replay. This is quantitative search progress only: no effective-layer UNSAT, repair-layer
    UNSAT or outer exhaustion was obtained. Round 63 adds a proposer-level library of 1,440 globally valid deletion-minimal
    five-atom single-residual order clauses, exhaustively replayed over 103,680 local-order cases with 7,200 atom-deletion
    controls. A separate ten-step experiment generated 15,874 witness-checked pair-pattern clauses but did not retain the
    raw ledgers needed for absence use. No checked DRUP/DRAT/LRAT proof exists, so the exact residual is unchanged. Round
    68 emits complete direct-360 DIMACS for one unrestricted clause-saturated candidate, but both sectors are SAT and raw
    replay to weight-10 Xerr and weight-8 Zerr mechanisms. Five continuation candidates are likewise killed in both sectors.
    Twelve new sound blocks are retained, for 1,472 total; no direct absence, repair entry or outer exhaustion exists, so
    D* remains in {11,12}. Round 70''s fresh exact review clears the round-69 positive-witness block library as proposer-level
    evidence and licenses strict continuation at outer iteration 6 with 6,212 installed sound blocks. It licenses no absence,

    24 outer candidates and all 48 complete direct sectors are SAT and raw-replayed, adding 48 candidate-exact blocks. The
    next reviewed-by-dispatcher frontier is iteration 30 with 6,260 blocks; no absence, repair, exhaustion or D* narrowing
    exists. Round 73''s fresh exact review clears the round-72 566-term conservative positive library and iterations 30--41
    as proposer-level evidence. The exact continuation frontier is iteration 42 with 6,850 installed blocks; no absence, repair,

    the sealed round-74 online generalization package as proposer-level evidence. Iterations 42--53 add 134 sound generalized
    terms and 24 candidate-exact blocks after twelve legal outer SAT schedules and twenty-four complete direct-sector SAT/raw
    replays. The exact fresh-process continuation is iteration 54 with 7,008 installed blocks: 1,440 unary, 20 round-62, 4,740
    round-69 generalized, 566 round-72 generalized, 134 round-74 generalized and 108 dynamic exact. The review literally replays
    all 1,137,096 retained pair tuples and validates the factorized exhaustive proof for 2,985,984 retained multi tuples.
    Deletion controls establish only candidate-relation or fixed-selector atom minimality, not installed-union or global physical-mechanism
    minimality. No absence, repair, exhaustion, D* narrowing, route change or level change is licensed. Round 77''s fresh
    independent exact review clears the sealed round-76 package as proposer-level evidence. Iterations 54--77 add 303 sound
    generalized terms and 48 candidate-exact blocks after twenty-four legal outer SAT schedules and forty-eight complete direct-sector
    SAT/raw replays. The exact fresh-process continuation is unstarted iteration 78 with G=5,743 generalized, D=156 dynamic
    exact, registry=72 and 7,359 installed blocks. The review literally replays all 2,637,648 retained pair tuples and validates

    legal outer schedules and twenty-four complete direct sectors are all SAT and raw-replayed, adding 24 candidate-exact
    blocks, 62 online terms and 1,964 further cross-registry terms. The review''s non-importing checker actually visits all
    43,834,872 literal selector tuples and validates four factorized exhaustive domains totaling 15,485,313,024 tuples, including
    classification, dominance, union and zero-domain checks. The exact fresh-process continuation is unstarted iteration 90

    81''s fresh independent exact review clears the sealed round-80 whole-registry package as proposer-level evidence. Its
    exact star synthesis retains 24 strict installed-union expansions, with all 304,704 literal raw-selector pair cells replayed
    and 35,294,400 factorized tuples checked. Iterations 90--101 contain twelve legal outer SAT schedules and twenty-four
    complete direct-sector SAT/raw replays, adding 94 online terms, 2,107 cross-registry terms and 24 candidate-exact blocks.
    The review independently visits all 20,820,216 online/cross literal tuples and validates 55,037,740,032 factorized tuples,
    plus the eight reconstructed multi-selector terms and their exact GF(2) domains. The exact fresh-process continuation
    is unstarted iteration 102 with G=12,820 generalized, D=204 dynamic exact, registry/raw=120 and 14,484 installed blocks.
    No direct query is UNSAT and no accepted absence proof exists, so no repair, exhaustion, D* narrowing, route change or
    level change is licensed. Round 93''s fresh independent exact review clears the sealed Round-92 weight-9 synthesis and
    finite-outer continuation package for proposer-level continuation only. Exact Phase-A enumeration retains 44 new ordinary
    EFSSCs and zero relaxed cubes, with an explicit strict-union expansion witness; iterations 124--135 are twelve legal outer
    SAT schedules with all twenty-four complete unrestricted direct-360 sectors SAT and physically replayed, including weight-9
    mechanisms at iteration 124 Zerr and iteration 133 Xerr. The atomic frontier is fresh/unstarted iteration 136 with the

    The review licenses no Gross UNSAT, direct absence, repair, exhaustion, D* narrowing, route promotion, or root promotion;

Round 95''s fresh independent exact review clears the sealed Round-94 package for proposer-level continuation only: iterations 136--147 add 24 exact EFSSC selectors after twelve legal outer SAT schedules and twenty-four unrestricted direct-sector SAT/physical replays; the complete same-sector order-3 GF(2) search retains zero terms; and the fresh frontier is unstarted iteration 148 with 256 selectors and checked-unsolved CNF sha256:397c12b15859a62bbe4bacbcb43dd8680a93e3bcf0cdee62fea4687d2431c8e4.
